Now let me think about the proof. This is a noninterference proof for STLC with security annotations. The standard approach is a logical relation (step-indexed? Here it's termination-insensitive so probably a logical relation over values).

Let me think about the structure.

Key definitions:
- security = {reader, indirect_reader}
- flows_to l1 l2 := False only when High -> Low.
- join l1 l2 := Low only if both Low.
- wf_security kappa := flows_to kappa.indirect_reader kappa.reader
- security_le kappa1 kappa2 := flows_to k1.reader k2.reader /\ flows_to k1.indirect_reader k2.indirect_reader
- protect_security kappa l := {reader := join kappa.reader l; indirect_reader := join kappa.indirect_reader l}
- Types: Ty_Unit kappa | Ty_Sum T1 T2 kappa | Ty_Prod T1 T2 kappa | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 kappa.
- ty_protect l T: protects outer security.
- wf_ty T: recursive well-formedness.
- Terms: standard, with tm_protect l t.
- Evaluation step: mostly standard. Note protection on results.
- T_If requires condition type Ty_Sum (Ty_Unit public) (Ty_Unit public) k, and result ty_protect k.indirect_reader T.

The theorem: closed t1, t2 of type T_secret. Context C typed from T_secret to T_result. ground T_result, transparent T_result, and ~ flows_to (security_of T_secret).indirect_reader (security_of T_result).indirect_reader. Then same_result (plug C t1) (plug C t2).

- T_Var: x : T in context. In the context lemma, we're plugging terms into C; the typing is of plug C (fvar x). Hmm.

Let me think about the structure of the type system and find the intended semantics.

Observations:
- Every elimination construct carries a label r and requires flows_to (eliminated value's outer security).reader r.
- The result of an elimination is protected by the eliminated value's outer security's indirect_reader.
- Protection joins both reader and indirect_reader with the label.
- `tm_protect l t` has type ty_protect l T; evaluation protects the value's outer annotation (when value).
- `transparent T` requires all nested types to have security ≤ outer.
- Ground excludes arrows.

Now, for the logical relation:
- `reader` = level of data: who may observe the value's contents.
- `indirect_reader` = level of computation/implicit flow that produced the value.

Let me define, for a level l:

`VR l T v1 v2` where v1, v2 are values (or arbitrary terms?). Let's define on values:
- Ty_Unit kappa: if flows_to kappa.indirect l then True else True (units erase equally always) — actually always True! Because erase of any unit value = tm_unit public. - Ty_Sum T1 T2 kappa: if flows_to kappa.indirect l then "v1 and v2 have the same constructor and their payloads related at the corresponding Ti at level l"... plus maybe require matching annotations? Erasure ignores annotations, so no.
  But hold on: if flows_to kappa.indirect l, do we require the constructors to match? For the theorem: T_result.indirect Low, l=Low → yes we require matching constructors. Good.
  If ¬ flows_to kappa.indirect l (i.e., kappa.indirect = High, l = Low): True (universal).
- Ty_Prod T1 T2 kappa: if flows_to kappa.indirect l then VR l T1 v1.1 v2.1 /\ VR l T2 v1.2 v2.2 else True.
- Ty_Arrow T1 T2 kappa: extensional: for all u1 u2, VR ? T1 u1 u2 -> TR ? T2 (app ...) ... where the level for argument and result must be determined by the function's annotations. Applying a function at label r requires flows_to kappa.reader r, and result protected by kappa.indirect. Hmm.

Hmm, what level should the arrow relation use? In classic NI logical relations, function relation at level l: related arguments at level l give related results at level l. But here the label r of the application site matters: the app's result type is protected by kappa.indirect_reader. Also `flows_to kappa.reader r` constrains where it can be applied.

Let's think about how T_App compatibility would go: Suppose f1 ≈ f2 at Arrow T1 T2 kappa at level l, and a1 ≈ a2 at T1 at level l. We need to show tm_app f1 a1 r ≈ tm_app f2 a2 r at ty_protect kappa.indirect T2 at level l, given flows_to kappa.reader r. VR l T v1 v2 :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Unit _ => True
  | Ty_Sum T1 T2 kappa =>
      if flows_to kappa.indirect_reader l then
        (exists j v1' v2', v1 = tm_inl v1' j /\ v2 = tm_inl v2' j /\ VR l T1 v1' v2')
        \/ (exists j v1' v2', v1 = tm_inr v1' j /\ v2 = tm_inr v2' j /\ VR l T2 v1' v2')
      else True
  | Ty_Prod T1 T2 kappa =>
      if flows_to kappa.indirect_reader l then
        exists j v1a v1b v2a v2b, v1 = tm_pair v1a v1b j /\ v2 = tm_pair v2a v2b j /\
          VR l T1 v1a v2a /\ VR l T2 v1b v2b
      else True
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 kappa => ... extensional ...
  end.

Simplification idea: For ground types, structural relation ≈ erasure equality (for sums, matching constructors and equal payload erasures = erasure equality; for products similarly; for units trivial). So define:

VR l T v1 v2 :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Unit _ => True
  | Ty_Sum T1 T2 kappa =>
      flows_to kappa.indirect l -> (erase v1 = erase v2)   (* structural equality for ground-ish sums *)
  | Ty_Prod T1 T2 kappa =>
      flows_to kappa.indirect l -> (erase v1 = erase v2)
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 kappa => extensional...
  end.

Hmm, but for Sum/Prod components that are arrow types (non-ground), erasure equality is too strong (closures with different bodies). So the relation must be structural with recursion at components, using the component relations (which for arrows is extensional). So can't use whole erasure equality for non-ground. But maybe we can define a "relation-level" recursion:

VR l T v1 v2 := 
  match T with
  | Ty_Unit _ => True
  | Ty_Sum T1 T2 kappa => (flows_to kappa.indirect l -> (structural: matching constructor and VR l Ti payload)) 
  | Ty_Prod T1 T2 kappa => (flows_to kappa.indirect l -> VR l T1 (fst components) /\ VR l T2 (snd))
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 kappa => extensional
  end.

where the "->" is implication (if the type's indirect flows to l, then structural conditions). That's the same as before.

Definitions:
```
Definition TR (l : label) (T : ty) (t1 t2 : tm) : Prop :=
  forall v1 v2, t1 -->* v1 -> t2 -->* v2 -> VR l T v1 v2.
```
with VR a Fixpoint on T:
```
Fixpoint VR (l : label) (T : ty) : tm -> tm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Unit _ => fun v1 v2 => True
  | Ty_Sum T1 T2 kappa => fun v1 v2 =>
      flows_to kappa.(indirect_reader) l ->
      (exists (j1 : security) (w1 : tm), v1 = tm_inl w1 j1 /\ ...) ...
    ...
```

- Sum: if flows_to kappa.ind l then (exists w1 w2, v1 = tm_inl w1 _ /\ v2 = tm_inl w2 _ /\ VR l T1 w1 w2) \/ (exists w1 w2, v1 = tm_inr w1 _ /\ v2 = tm_inr w2 _ /\ VR l T2 w1 w2). But VR is a Fixpoint over T applied to terms; we can state it for arbitrary terms but only use values. The sum case pattern-matches on v1, v2 structurally: better to match on the terms:
```
| Ty_Sum T1 T2 kappa => fun v1 v2 =>
    flows_to kappa.(indirect_reader) l ->
    match v1, v2 with
    | tm_inl w1 _, tm_inl w2 _ => VR l T1 w1 w2
    | tm_inr w1 _, tm_inr w2 _ => VR l T2 w1 w2
    | _, _ => False
    end
```
This works structurally (Fixpoint on T with v1, v2 as arguments; the recursion is on T, fine).

As noted, transparent T_result gives: for every nested type U (including T_result), security_le (security_of U) (security_of T_result). Let me define a recursive predicate:

```
Fixpoint all_indirect_flows (l : label) (T : ty) : Prop :=
  flows_to (security_of T).(indirect_reader) l /\
  match T with
  | Ty_Unit _ => True
  | Ty_Sum T1 T2 _ | Ty_Prod T1 T2 _ => all_indirect_flows l T1 /\ all_indirect_flows l T2
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 _ => all_indirect_flows l T1 /\ all_indirect_flows l T2  (* arrows excluded by ground anyway *)
  end.
```

From transparent T_result and T_result.ind = Low, prove all_indirect_flows Low T_result. transparent_at kappa T gives security_le (security_of T) kappa (for the top) and recursively transparent_at kappa T1, T2. So for each nested U: security_le (security_of U) kappa where kappa = security_of T_result. Thus flows_to U.ind T_result.ind = Low, and Low → Low ✓. So all nested U have indirect Low. So all_indirect_flows Low T_result ✓. Proof by induction on T.

Now let's carefully define the relation and check the fundamental theorem statement's well-foundedness. VR is a Fixpoint on ty with TR used inside VR's Arrow branch. TR is defined in terms of VR (using evaluates). This is a mutual recursion between VR (structural on T) and TR (a definitional wrapper). In Coq, we can define TR first? TR uses VR; VR uses TR. Mutual Fixpoint? VR's recursion is on T; TR is not recursive. We can define:

```
Fixpoint VR (l : label) (T : ty) (v1 v2 : tm) {struct T} : Prop :=
  match T with
  ...
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 kappa =>
      flows_to kappa.(indirect_reader) l ->
      match v1, v2 with
      | tm_abs _ b1 _, tm_abs _ b2 _ =>
          forall u1 u2, VR l T1 u1 u2 -> TR l T2 (open b1 u1) (open b2 u2)
      | _, _ => False
      end
  end
with TR (l : label) (T : ty) (t1 t2 : tm) {struct T} : Prop :=
  forall v1 v2, evaluates t1 v1 -> evaluates t2 v2 -> VR l T v1 v2.
```
Mutual fixpoint with TR structurally on... TR doesn't recurse on T; it calls VR l T. In a mutual Fixpoint, Coq requires the recursion to decrease; VR calls TR l T2 where T2 is a subterm of T ✓; TR calls VR l T (same T) — is that allowed? In mutual fixpoints, calls between the two must be on structurally smaller arguments of the *same* mutual block with the same principal argument? Coq's guard checker for mutual fixpoints: a call from f to g must be with an argument that is structurally smaller than f's principal argument (for the recursive call to decrease). Actually the rule: in a mutual fixpoint, calls to any function in the block must have the principal argument structurally smaller than the caller's principal argument (except when the caller is not recursing?). Hmm, TR calls VR l T where T is TR's own principal argument — that's *equal*, not smaller, so the guard condition fails (the combined call graph TR -> VR with equal T, and VR -> TR with smaller T2; a cycle TR -> VR (equal) -> TR (smaller)? Not a direct cycle on equal args, but the checker requires each call to decrease). I think Coq's guard checker will reject TR calling VR on the same argument because it can't prove termination of the mutual block (the call TR l T -> VR l T -> TR l T2... wait VR l T calls TR l T2 with T2 smaller, so overall it's fine? But the checker's rule is local: every call in the block must have the principal argument smaller than the caller's, I believe. Since TR -> VR with equal arg wouldn't satisfy that.

Simplest fix: don't make TR a Fixpoint; define TR after VR, but VR needs TR. Hmm, circular. Options:
1. Use a nested Fixpoint / define VR with a local `tr` abbreviation? In Coq, you can define VR as a Fixpoint and inside use a `let` for TR? TR needs to be recursive? No, TR is just `forall v1 v2, evaluates ... -> VR l T v1 v2`. Inside VR's definition, for the Arrow case we need TR l T2 for the *smaller* T2. We can define a local notation: for the Fixpoint body, we can write the unfolded form directly:
```
| Ty_Arrow T1 T2 kappa =>
    flows_to kappa.ind l ->
    match v1, v2 with
    | tm_abs _ b1 _, tm_abs _ b2 _ =>
        forall u1 u2, VR l T1 u1 u2 ->
          forall w1 w2, evaluates (open b1 u1) w1 -> evaluates (open b2 u2) w2 -> VR l T2 w1 w2
    | _, _ => False
    end
```
This inlines TR, and VR is a plain Fixpoint on T. Then define TR separately as the wrapper. But then the lemmas about TR (protection, subtype monotonicity, etc.) need to match the inlined form in the Arrow case. E.g., the Arrow VR extensional clause expects `TR l T2 (open b1 u1) (open b2 u2)`, which after unfolding TR is exactly the inlined form ✓ (delta-reduction). When proving things about VR (like VR_mono by induction on subtype), we unfold the definitions and the inlined form matches TR's definition by reflexivity? TR l T2 a b = (forall v1 v2, evaluates a v1 -> evaluates b v2 -> VR l T2 v1 v2), while the inlined form has `forall u1 u2, VR l T1 u1 u2 -> forall w1 w2, evaluates ... -> ... -> VR l T2 w1 w2`. If we state the extensional requirement using TR, the definitional unfolding TR gives the same shape ✓ (as a Prop unfolding, `change` or `unfold TR` works).

2. Alternatively use a Definition with a well-founded measure or define VR with `T` and use `let TR := ...` locally where the recursion is on T2 — but Coq's guard checker might complain about the local definition. Actually, a cleaner approach: define VR and TR *not* mutually, using the fact that TR doesn't need to be recursive if we inline. Option 1 is fine.

Alternatively, use a "Section" with a Fixpoint VR taking TR as... no.

Option 1 details: Write VR as a single Fixpoint with the arrow case inlining TR. Then define TR after. All subsequent lemmas use TR. When we unfold VR's Arrow case, we'll get the inlined form; `unfold TR` will convert the goal's TR into the same shape. Since VR's recursive calls are on T1 and T2 (subterms) ✓, guard passes.

Hmm, one concern: in the Arrow branch, the recursive calls VR l T1 and VR l T2 are under a match on v1, v2 and a forall; that's fine for the guard checker.

Let me now write the definitions:

```coq
Definition flows_to (l1 l2 : label) : Prop := ... (given)

Fixpoint VR (l : label) (T : ty) (v1 v2 : tm) {struct T} : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Unit kappa =>
      flows_to kappa.(indirect_reader) l -> erase_security v1 = erase_security v2
  | Ty_Sum T1 T2 kappa =>
      flows_to kappa.(indirect_reader) l ->
      match v1, v2 with
      | tm_inl w1 _, tm_inl w2 _ => VR l T1 w1 w2
      | tm_inr w1 _, tm_inr w2 _ => VR l T2 w1 w2
      | _, _ => False
      end
  | Ty_Prod T1 T2 kappa =>
      flows_to kappa.(indirect_reader) l ->
      match v1, v2 with
      | tm_pair a1 b1 _, tm_pair a2 b2 _ => VR l T1 a1 a2 /\ VR l T2 b1 b2
      | _, _ => False
      end
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 kappa =>
      flows_to kappa.(indirect_reader) l ->
      match v1, v2 with
      | tm_abs _ b1 _, tm_abs _ b2 _ =>
          forall u1 u2, VR l T1 u1 u2 ->
            forall w1 w2, evaluates (open b1 u1) w1 -> evaluates (open b2 u2) w2 -> VR l T2 w1 w2
      | _, _ => False
      end
  end.

Definition TR (l : label) (T : ty) (t1 t2 : tm) : Prop :=
  forall v1 v2, evaluates t1 v1 -> evaluates t2 v2 -> VR l T v1 v2.
```

Note: `evaluates` is defined before (in the input file it's after multi, line 273) ✓. And `erase_security` is defined at line 196 before step ✓. Good, both available before the theorem. We'll append our definitions after the supplied definitions (before the theorem) — but we must not modify supplied definitions; adding after line 404 (before Theorem) is fine. Actually we can also add after the theorem? We need to replace `Admitted`. We can put our lemmas before the theorem and then prove the theorem. Since the file is "self-contained", we can restructure: insert definitions/lemmas before the Theorem statement? The theorem statement must remain unchanged; we can put lemmas before it (after line 404). Yes.

Fixpoint subst_atoms (s : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => s x
  | tm_unit k => tm_unit k

Fixpoint fv_context (C : program_context) : list atom :=

Lemma multi_protect_inv : forall j t v, multi (tm_protect j t) v -> value v ->

Lemma multi_app_inv : forall f a r v,

    multi f fv /\ value fv /\ fv = tm_abs T body k /\

No: if f is a value, f -> f' requires values to step, impossible. So ST_App1 applies as long as f can step. So the decomposition holds: f reaches an abs value, a reaches a value, ST_AppAbs fires, then the protected body continues to v.

But careful about the ORDER: ST_App2 steps a while f is a value (the abs). Then ST_AppAbs when a is a value. So multi f fv where fv = abs, and multi a av. ✓.

So we need inversion on step with the app shape. Then in the ST_App1/2 cases, the resulting `fv` from IH is an abs etc. ✓.

**Fundamental theorem**
```
Lemma fundamental : forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall l (s1 s2 : atom -> tm),
    (forall x U, lookup_context x Gamma = Some U -> TR l U (s1 x) (s2 x)) ->
    TR l T (subst_atoms s1 t) (subst_atoms s2 t).
```
Proof by induction on has_type. Careful: the T_Abs and T_Case rules have a premise `forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type ... (open body (fvar x)) ...`. To use the IH, we need to instantiate x with a fresh variable and apply the IH to that instance. Since the induction hypothesis is `forall x, ~ In x L -> TR l T2 (...)`, we can instantiate with our chosen fresh x ✓.

But the IH's statement includes the s1 s2 quantification, and the env relation. When we apply IH to the subderivation at x (with its specific s1', s2'), the IH gives the TR for that x ✓.

Now, in the T_Abs case, we need the equality subst_atoms s1' (open body (fvar x)) = open (subst_atoms s1 body) u1 as discussed, requiring x ∉ fv body. Choose x fresh w.r.t. L ∪ fv body ∪ ... hmm, also x must be such that x ∉ L. Fine.

**Substitution environment details**
The env relation is `forall x U, lookup_context x Gamma = Some U -> TR l U (s1 x) (s2 x)`. For T_Var, the typing gives lookup x Gamma = Some T and wf_ty T; we need TR l T (s1 x) (s2 x) ✓ directly.

- `lookup_update_neq : x ≠ y -> lookup_context y (update Gamma x T) = lookup_context y Gamma`.

But there's a subtlety in the extension env: for y = x, lookup x (update Gamma x T1) = Some T1. But also, if Gamma contains x, lookup x (update Gamma x T1) is still Some T1 (head) ✓.

**Final theorem**
```
Theorem noninterference : ...

  intros. 
  destruct (not_flows_inv H4) as [Hs_ind [Hr_ind ...]]  (* T_secret.ind = High, T_result.ind = Low *)

Steps:
1. From H5 : ~ flows_to secret.ind result.ind, derive Hsec : (security_of T_secret).indirect_reader = High and Hres : (security_of T_result).indirect_reader = Low.

Lemma VR_vacuous : forall l T, ~ flows_to (security_of T).(indirect_reader) l -> forall v1 v2, VR l T v1 v2.

```

Lemma VR_vacuous : forall l T, ~ flows_to (security_of T).(indirect_reader) l -> forall v1 v2, VR l T v1 v2.

```
Lemma transparent_all_indirect : forall T, transparent T -> all_indirect_flows (security_of T).(indirect_reader) T.

Let's prove by induction on T: transparent T = transparent_at (security_of T) T. For Unit: transparent_at k (Ty_Unit k) = security_le k k /\ True. ```
Fixpoint all_le (kappa : security) (T : ty) : Prop :=
  security_le (security_of T) kappa /\
  match T with
  | Ty_Unit _ => True
  | Ty_Sum T1 T2 _ | Ty_Prod T1 T2 _ | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 _ => all_le kappa T1 /\ all_le kappa T2
  end.
```
Then `transparent_at k T <-> all_le k T`? Let's check the definitions: transparent_at kappa T := security_le (security_of T) kappa /\ match T with ... => transparent_at kappa T1 /\ transparent_at kappa T2 end. And all_le kappa T := security_le (security_of T) kappa /\ (recursive all_le). They're the same shape! So `transparent_at k T <-> all_le k T` by induction (they're literally defined the same way, modulo naming). We can prove `transparent_at_all_le` by induction on T (or just note they're convertible? Their Fixpoints are structurally identical: transparent_at kappa T = security_le (security_of T) kappa /\ match T with Unit => True | Sum T1 T2 _ => transparent_at kappa T1 /\ transparent_at kappa T2 ... end. all_le same with all_le. They're different fixpoints but identical structure, so mutual implication by induction ✓).

Then `all_indirect_flows l T` from `all_le kappa T` and `flows_to kappa.ind l`: for each nested U, flows_to U.ind kappa.ind (from security_le) and kappa.ind → l, so flows_to U.ind l by transitivity ✓. Prove by induction on T.

In the theorem: transparent T_result gives transparent_at (security_of T_result) T_result, hence all_le (security_of T_result) T_result; with Hres: security_of T_result.ind = Low, so flows_to (security_of T_result).ind Low ✓ (that's flows_to Low Low = True); then all_indirect_flows Low T_result ✓.

8. The erasure lemma:
```
Lemma VR_erase : forall l T, ground T -> all_indirect_flows l T -> forall v1 v2, VR l T v1 v2 -> erase_security v1 = erase_security v2.

Proof by induction on T:

  - mismatches: the match reduces to False → contradiction.

OK, so maybe has_type does NOT imply wf_ty T, and the T_App output can have deep non-wf components. Does that break our proof? Let's check where we need wf of the static type annotations.

In the fundamental theorem, the relation VR uses only the type structure and its annotations; the proofs only need:
- flows_to properties (from subtype).
- The guard reasoning: if ¬ flows_to kappa.ind l then target guard fails: this needs join properties, no wf needed.
- VR_vacuous: uses ¬flows_to directly, no wf.
- VR_protect_value: no wf.
- VR_sub: subtype gives security_le which gives the needed flows; no wf.
- T_App: needs the static kappa.ind for the protection; no wf.
- T_If: needs the condition type Ty_Sum (Ty_Unit public) (Ty_Unit public) k; the target ty_protect k.ind T; the case analysis on ¬flows_to k.ind l. No wf.

Let's test whether deep non-wf types can be derived. Start with T_Abs: Arrow T1 T2 kappa with wf_ty T1 (given), wf_security kappa, and T2 from body typing. Actually, let me think about whether to use `Program`/`Fixpoint` with the match on two tm's in the Sum case. In Coq, a Fixpoint on T with a match on v1, v2 inside is fine (the recursive calls are on T1/T2 which are subterms of T — the guard checker looks at the principal argument T). However, the nested match on v1/v2 returning Prop with recursive occurrences VR l T1 ... — the guard checker requires the recursive calls' principal arguments to be subterms of the original T. T1 is a subterm of `Ty_Sum T1 T2 kappa`, but does the guard checker see the match on T first? The Fixpoint is `Fixpoint VR (l : label) (T : ty) (v1 v2 : tm) {struct T}`, matching on T first, then nested matches on v1/v2 — the recursive calls happen inside the branch for Ty_Sum with T1 which is a subterm of the matched T ✓. Should be fine.

But hmm, there's a subtlety: the guard checker needs the recursive argument to be a variable or subterm; `T1` is a pattern variable from matching T = Ty_Sum T1 T2 kappa ✓.

Let me write:

```coq
Fixpoint VR (l : label) (T : ty) (v1 v2 : tm) {struct T} : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Unit kappa =>
      flows_to kappa.(indirect_reader) l -> erase_security v1 = erase_security v2
  | Ty_Sum T1 T2 kappa =>
      flows_to kappa.(indirect_reader) l ->
        match v1, v2 with
        | tm_inl w1 _, tm_inl w2 _ => VR l T1 w1 w2
        | tm_inr w1 _, tm_inr w2 _ => VR l T2 w1 w2
        | _, _ => False
        end
  | Ty_Prod T1 T2 kappa =>
      flows_to kappa.(indirect_reader) l ->
        match v1, v2 with
        | tm_pair a1 b1 _, tm_pair a2 b2 _ => VR l T1 a1 a2 /\ VR l T2 b1 b2
        | _, _ => False
        end
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 kappa =>
      flows_to kappa.(indirect_reader) l ->
        match v1, v2 with
        | tm_abs _ b1 _, tm_abs _ b2 _ =>
            forall u1 u2, value u1 -> value u2 -> VR l T1 u1 u2 ->
              forall w1 w2, evaluates (open b1 u1) w1 -> evaluates (open b2 u2) w2 ->
                VR l T2 w1 w2
        | _, _ => False
        end
  end.
```

Wait, there's an issue: `VR` is defined after `evaluates`? The input file defines `evaluates` at line 273, before subtype/has_type. We'll add our definitions after line 404 (after `transparent`/`same_result`? Actually same_result is at 401-404 and the theorem at 406). We can insert after line 404. `evaluates` and `erase_security` are available ✓. `value` available ✓.

Then:
```
Definition TR (l : label) (T : ty) (t1 t2 : tm) : Prop :=
  forall v1 v2, evaluates t1 v1 -> evaluates t2 v2 -> VR l T v1 v2.
```

Let me now also plan `subst_atoms` and `fv` lemmas:

```
Fixpoint subst_atoms (s : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => s x
  | tm_unit k => tm_unit k
  | tm_abs T body k => tm_abs T (subst_atoms s body) k
  | tm_app t1 t2 r => tm_app (subst_atoms s t1) (subst_atoms s t2) r
  | tm_pair t1 t2 k => tm_pair (subst_atoms s t1) (subst_atoms s t2) k
  | tm_fst t1 r => tm_fst (subst_atoms s t1) r
  | tm_snd t1 r => tm_snd (subst_atoms s t1) r
  | tm_inl t1 k => tm_inl (subst_atoms s t1) k
  | tm_inr t1 k => tm_inr (subst_atoms s t1) k
  | tm_case t0 b1 b2 r => tm_case (subst_atoms s t0) (subst_atoms s b1) (subst_atoms s b2) r
  | tm_protect l t1 => tm_protect l (subst_atoms s t1)
  | tm_if c y n r => tm_if (subst_atoms s c) (subst_atoms s y) (subst_atoms s n) r
  end.
```
Note: this definition is structurally similar to the supplied `subst` and could just use it: `subst x u t`. But for environments we need a function. Fine.

Now the TR protection lemma:
```
Lemma TR_protect : forall l T t1 t2 j j1 j2,
  TR l T t1 t2 -> TR l (ty_protect j T) (tm_protect j1 t1) (tm_protect j2 t2).
Proof.
  intros l T t1 t2 j j1 j2 H. unfold TR in *.
  intros v1 v2 [Hm1 Hval1] [Hm2 Hval2].
  apply multi_protect_inv in Hm1 as (w1 & Hm1' & Hvalw1 & Heq1); [| exact Hval1].
  ...
  subst v1 v2.
  apply VR_protect_value; [| exact j1 | exact j2].
  apply H; split; assumption.
Qed.
```
Where multi_protect_inv : multi (tm_protect j t) v -> value v -> exists w, multi t w /\ value w /\ v = protect_value w j. Note the protection label in the decomposition matches the term's label j1 ✓.

Given Hfun : TR l (Ty_Arrow T1 T2 kappa) f1 f2.

Lemma lc_open_rec : forall t m k u, lc_at m t -> m <= k -> open_rec k u t = t.

  intros t. induction t; intros m k u H Hle; simpl in *.

Actually, here's a cleaner idea: prove a helper lemma that directly handles the "forall x fresh" pattern:

```
Lemma lc_at_open_fresh : forall (body : tm) (L : list atom) (k : nat),
  (forall x, ~ In x L -> lc_at k (open body (tm_fvar x))) ->
  lc_at (S k) body.
Proof.
  intros body L k H.
  pose proof (H (fresh_atom L) (fresh_atom_spec L)).
  apply lc_at_open_rec_S with (k := k) (u := tm_fvar (fresh_atom L)).
  - exact H0.  (* hmm, open = open_rec 0, but we need open_rec k... *)
```
Wait, lc_at_open_rec_S requires lc_at k (open_rec k u body), but the hypothesis gives lc_at k (open body (fvar x)) = lc_at k (open_rec 0 (fvar x) body). If k ≠ 0, these differ. In our use k = 0 ✓. So:

```
Lemma lc_body_of_open : forall body L,
  (forall x, ~ In x L -> lc_at 0 (open body (tm_fvar x))) -> lc_at 1 body.
Proof.
  intros body L H.
  pose proof (H (fresh_atom L) (fresh_atom_spec L)) as Hx.
  apply lc_at_open_rec_S with (k := 0) (u := tm_fvar (fresh_atom L)).
  - exact Hx.
  - exact I.
Qed.
```

Also, I need to double check VR's Arrow branch definition regarding `value` premises and the body quantification. Let me define:

```
Fixpoint VR (l : label) (T : ty) (v1 v2 : tm) {struct T} : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Unit kappa =>
      flows_to kappa.(indirect_reader) l ->
      erase_security v1 = erase_security v2
  | Ty_Sum T1 T2 kappa =>
      flows_to kappa.(indirect_reader) l ->
      match v1, v2 with
      | tm_inl w1 _, tm_inl w2 _ => VR l T1 w1 w2
      | tm_inr w1 _, tm_inr w2 _ => VR l T2 w1 w2
      | _, _ => False
      end
  | Ty_Prod T1 T2 kappa =>
      flows_to kappa.(indirect_reader) l ->
      match v1, v2 with
      | tm_pair a1 b1 _, tm_pair a2 b2 _ => VR l T1 a1 a2 /\ VR l T2 b1 b2
      | _, _ => False
      end
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 kappa =>
      flows_to kappa.(indirect_reader) l ->
      match v1, v2 with
      | tm_abs _ b1 _, tm_abs _ b2 _ =>
          forall (u1 u2 : tm), value u1 -> value u2 -> VR l T1 u1 u2 ->
            forall (w1 w2 : tm), evaluates (open b1 u1) w1 -> evaluates (open b2 u2) w2 ->
              VR l T2 w1 w2
      | _, _ => False
      end
  end.

Definition TR (l : label) (T : ty) (t1 t2 : tm) : Prop :=
  forall v1 v2 : tm, evaluates t1 v1 -> evaluates t2 v2 -> VR l T v1 v2.
```

Let me add these after the has_type_locally_closed lemmas, then the relation lemmas.

```
Lemma multi_app_inv : forall f a r v,
  multi (tm_app f a r) v -> value v ->
  exists (fv av : tm) (Ta : ty) (body : tm) (k : security),
    multi f fv /\ value fv /\ fv = tm_abs Ta body k /\
    multi a av /\ value av /\ flows_to k.(reader) r /\
    multi (tm_protect k.(indirect_reader) (open body av)) v.
Proof.
  intros f a r v Hm. remember (tm_app f a r) as s eqn:Es.
  revert f a r Es. induction Hm; intros f0 a0 r0 Es Hval.
  - exfalso. rewrite <- Es in Hval. simpl in Hval. exact (value_app f0 a0 r0 Hval).
    (* Hmm, in the refl case, the goal's value is s; Es : tm_app f0 a0 r0 = s; Hval : value s; rewrite <- Es in Hval gives value (tm_app f0 a0 r0) ✓ *)
  - rewrite Es in Hstep.

Lemma step_app_inv : forall f a r t,
  tm_app f a r --> t ->
  (exists T body k v, f = tm_abs T body k /\ a = v /\
     value (tm_abs T body k) /\ value v /\ flows_to k.(reader) r /\
     t = tm_protect k.(indirect_reader) (open body v))
  \/ (exists f', f --> f' /\ t = tm_app f' a r)
  \/ (exists a', value f /\ a --> a' /\ t = tm_app f a' r).
Proof.
  intros f a r t H. inversion H; subst; eauto 12.
Qed.
```

Lemma multi_case_inv : forall t b1 b2 r v,
  multi (tm_case t b1 b2 r) v -> value v ->
  (exists w k, multi t (tm_inl w k) /\ value w /\ flows_to k.(reader) r /\
     multi (tm_protect k.(indirect_reader) (open b1 w)) v)

Excellent! Part 8 compiles. Now the biggest part: the fundamental theorem.

Let me design the statement:

```coq
Definition env_rel (l : label) (Gamma : context) (s1 s2 : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  forall (x : atom) (U : ty), lookup_context x Gamma = Some U -> TR l U (s1 x) (s2 x).

Definition env_lc (s : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  forall x, locally_closed (s x).

Lemma fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall l (s1 s2 : atom -> tm),
    env_lc s1 -> env_lc s2 -> env_rel l Gamma s1 s2 ->
    TR l T (subst_atoms s1 t) (subst_atoms s2 t).
```

Proof by induction on the typing derivation. I'll use `induction H` and refer to the hypotheses via match goal / auto. Let me plan each case:

Because the IH for binders is over `forall x, ~ In x L -> TR ... (subst_atoms s1 (open body (fvar x))) ...` given env_lc and env_rel of the *extended* context — I need to instantiate at a fresh x and provide extended substitutions.

The general proof structure per case:

**T_Var**: lookup x Gamma = Some T. Then TR l T (s1 x) (s2 x) from env_rel ✓ (subst_atoms s1 (fvar x) = s1 x).

**T_Unit**: term tm_unit kappa. subst = tm_unit kappa. TR: given evaluates (tm_unit kappa) v1: v1 = tm_unit kappa (values don't step); similarly v2; need VR l (Ty_Unit kappa) (tm_unit kappa) (tm_unit kappa) = flows_to ... -> erase equal ✓ reflexivity.

Then VR: simpl; intro; reflexivity.

Let me now write the fundamental theorem. I'll also need the env lemmas:
```
Lemma lookup_update_eq : forall x Gamma T, lookup_context x (update Gamma x T) = Some T.
Lemma lookup_update_neq : forall x y Gamma T, x <> y -> lookup_context y (update Gamma x T) = lookup_context y Gamma.
```
And the env_rel extension lemma:
```
Lemma env_rel_update : forall l Gamma s1 s2 x T u1 u2,
  env_rel l Gamma s1 s2 ->
  TR l T u1 u2 ->
  env_rel l (update Gamma x T) (extend x u1 s1) (extend x u2 s2).
Proof.
  intros l Gamma s1 s2 x T u1 u2 Henv HT y U Hlk.
  destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
  - apply Nat.eqb_eq in E; subst y.
    rewrite lookup_update_eq in Hlk. injection Hlk as ->.
    rewrite extend_eq. rewrite extend_eq. exact HT.
  - apply Nat.eqb_neq in E.
    rewrite lookup_update_neq in Hlk by auto.
    rewrite (extend_neq x y u1 s1) by auto.
    rewrite (extend_neq x y u2 s2) by auto.
    apply Henv. exact Hlk.
Qed.
```
Careful: lookup_update_eq's statement uses eqb y x internally; if x = y then lookup y (update Gamma x T) = lookup y (update Gamma y T) = Some T ✓ by lookup_update_eq with x := y.

Hmm, lookup_update_eq : forall x Gamma T, lookup_context x (update Gamma x T) = Some T. With y = x ✓.

Also env_lc extension:
```
Lemma env_lc_extend : forall s x u, env_lc s -> locally_closed u -> env_lc (extend x u s).
Proof.
  intros s x u Hlc Hu y. unfold extend. destruct (Nat.eqb x y).
  - exact Hu.
  - apply Hlc.
Qed.
```

Lemma multi_abs_eq : forall T body k v, multi (tm_abs T body k) v -> v = tm_abs T body k.
Proof.
  intros T body k v Hm. remember (tm_abs T body k) as s eqn:Es. revert T body k Es.

  - (* refl *) rewrite <- Es. reflexivity.  (* v = s = abs *)
  - (* step: s --> t2, but no step from tm_abs *) 
    rewrite <- Es in H. inversion H.  (* no cases *)
Qed.
```

```
Lemma lookup_update_neq : forall x y Gamma T,
  x <> y -> lookup_context y (update Gamma x T) = lookup_context y Gamma.
Proof.
  intros x y Gamma T H. unfold update, lookup_context. simpl.
  destruct (Nat.eqb y x) eqn:E.
  - apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. exfalso. apply H. symmetry. exact E.
  - reflexivity.
Qed.
```

Very interesting: with `as [ | m IHm | P HP ]`, the third case has:
- P : nat -> Prop
- HP : forall x, foo x (the premise)
- H : nat -> True (the generated IH named H by Coq, not by my pattern)

So the generated IH for a forall-premise is auto-named H, and my pattern name HP captured the premise. To name the IH, I'd need... the pattern has one name per argument, and the IH gets an auto name. Since the auto name is H here, and in the T_Abs case it was also H, I can rely on `H` being the generated IH? But "H" may clash with other hypotheses. In the real has_type induction, other hypotheses might be named H too; Coq will pick H0, H1, etc.

Better approach: use the fact that the generated IH is the *only* hypothesis matching the shape `forall x, ~ In x L -> TR ...`. Use `match goal` to capture it. This is reliable.

So for the fundamental theorem, let me use `as` patterns with explicit IH names. Let me carefully construct the patterns for each has_type constructor, listing constructor arguments and IH names after recursive occurrences:

Constructors and their arguments (from the inductive definition):

1. T_Var : forall Gamma x T, lookup_context x Gamma = Some T -> wf_ty T -> has_type Gamma (tm_fvar x) T.
   Args: Gamma, x, T, Hlk, Hwf. No recursive args. Pattern: `Gamma x T Hlk Hwf`.

2. T_Unit : forall Gamma kappa, wf_security kappa -> has_type ... 
   Pattern: `Gamma kappa Hwf`.

3. T_Abs : forall L Gamma T1 body T2 kappa, wf_ty T1 -> wf_security kappa -> (forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open body (fvar x)) T2) -> has_type Gamma (tm_abs T1 body kappa) (Arrow T1 T2 kappa).
   Args: L, Gamma, T1, body, T2, kappa, HwfT1, Hwfkappa, Premise(forall x...). IH for premise.
   Pattern: `L Gamma T1 body T2 kappa HwfT1 Hwfkappa Hbody IHbody`.

4. T_App : forall Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 kappa r, has_type Gamma t1 (Arrow T1 T2 kappa) -> has_type Gamma t2 T1 -> flows_to kappa.reader r -> has_type Gamma (tm_app t1 t2 r) (ty_protect kappa.ind T2).
   Args: Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 kappa r H1 (rec) H2 (rec) Hflow. IHs after each recursive arg.
   Pattern: `Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 kappa r H1 IH1 H2 IH2 Hflow`.

5. T_Pair : forall Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 kappa, has_type Gamma t1 T1 -> has_type Gamma t2 T2 -> wf_security kappa -> has_type Gamma (tm_pair t1 t2 kappa) (Ty_Prod T1 T2 kappa).
   Pattern: `Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 kappa H1 IH1 H2 IH2 Hwf`.

6. T_Fst : forall Gamma t T1 T2 kappa r, has_type Gamma t (Ty_Prod T1 T2 kappa) -> flows_to kappa.reader r -> has_type Gamma (tm_fst t r) (ty_protect kappa.ind T1).
   Pattern: `Gamma t T1 T2 kappa r H1 IH1 Hflow`.

7. T_Snd : same with T2: `Gamma t T1 T2 kappa r H1 IH1 Hflow`.

8. T_Inl : forall Gamma t T1 T2 kappa, has_type Gamma t T1 -> wf_ty T2 -> wf_security kappa -> has_type Gamma (tm_inl t kappa) (Ty_Sum T1 T2 kappa).
   Pattern: `Gamma t T1 T2 kappa H1 IH1 HwfT2 Hwf`.

9. T_Inr : forall Gamma t T1 T2 kappa, wf_ty T1 -> has_type Gamma t T2 -> wf_security kappa -> has_type Gamma (tm_inr t kappa) (Ty_Sum T1 T2 kappa).
   Pattern: `Gamma t T1 T2 kappa HwfT1 H1 IH1 Hwf`.

10. T_Case : forall L Gamma t body1 body2 T1 T2 T kappa r, has_type Gamma t (Ty_Sum T1 T2 kappa) -> flows_to kappa.reader r -> (forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open body1 (fvar x)) T) -> (forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (update Gamma x T2) (open body2 (fvar x)) T) -> has_type Gamma (tm_case t body1 body2 r) (ty_protect kappa.ind T).
    Args: L Gamma t body1 body2 T1 T2 T kappa r H1 (rec) Hflow H2 (forall premise, rec) H3 (forall premise, rec).
    Pattern: `L Gamma t body1 body2 T1 T2 T kappa r H1 IH1 Hflow H2 IH2 H3 IH3`.

11. T_Protect : forall Gamma t T l, has_type Gamma t T -> has_type Gamma (tm_protect l t) (ty_protect l T).
    Pattern: `Gamma t T l H1 IH1`.

12. T_Sub : forall Gamma t T U, has_type Gamma t T -> subtype T U -> has_type Gamma t U.
    Pattern: `Gamma t T U H1 IH1 Hsub`.

13. T_If : forall Gamma c yes no k T r, has_type Gamma c (Sum Unit Unit k) -> has_type Gamma yes T -> has_type Gamma no T -> flows_to k.reader r -> has_type Gamma (tm_if c yes no r) (ty_protect k.ind T).
    Pattern: `Gamma c yes no k T r H1 IH1 H2 IH2 H3 IH3 Hflow`.

```coq
Lemma fundamental : forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall l s1 s2,
    env_lc s1 -> env_lc s2 -> env_rel l Gamma s1 s2 ->
    TR l T (subst_atoms s1 t) (subst_atoms s2 t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T Hty.
  induction Hty as
    [ Gamma x T Hlk Hwf
    | Gamma kappa Hwf
    | L Gamma T1 body T2 kappa HwfT1 Hwfkappa Hbody IHbody
    | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 kappa r H1 IH1 H2 IH2 Hflow
    | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 kappa H1 IH1 H2 IH2 Hwf
    | Gamma t T1 T2 kappa r H1 IH1 Hflow
    | Gamma t T1 T2 kappa r H1 IH1 Hflow
    | Gamma t T1 T2 kappa H1 IH1 HwfT2 Hwf
    | Gamma t T1 T2 kappa HwfT1 H1 IH1 Hwf
    | L Gamma t body1 body2 T1 T2 T kappa r H1 IH1 Hflow H2 IH2 H3 IH3
    | Gamma t T l H1 IH1
    | Gamma t T U H1 IH1 Hsub
    | Gamma c yes no k T r H1 IH1 H2 IH2 H3 IH3 Hflow ];
    intros l s1 s2 Hlc1 Hlc2 Henv.
  - (* T_Var *) simpl. eapply Henv. eassumption.
  - (* T_Unit *) simpl. unfold TR. intros v1 v2 [Hm1 Hv1] [Hm2 Hv2].
    rewrite (multi_unit_eq kappa v1 Hm1). rewrite (multi_unit_eq kappa v2 Hm2).
    simpl. intro. reflexivity.
  - (* T_Abs *) ...
```

**fv_context** and **plug_subst**:

```
Fixpoint fv_context (C : program_context) : list atom :=
  match C with
  | C_Hole => []
  | C_Abs _ C' _ => fv_context C'
  | C_AppLeft C' t _ => fv_context C' ++ fv t
  | C_AppRight t C' _ => fv t ++ fv_context C'
  | C_PairLeft C' t _ => fv_context C' ++ fv t
  | C_PairRight t C' _ => fv t ++ fv_context C'
  | C_Fst C' _ | C_Snd C' _ | C_Inl C' _ | C_Inr C' _ | C_Protect _ C' => fv_context C'
  | C_CaseScrutinee C' b1 b2 _ => fv_context C' ++ fv b1 ++ fv b2
  | C_CaseLeft t0 C' b2 _ => fv t0 ++ fv_context C' ++ fv b2
  | C_CaseRight t0 b1 C' _ => fv t0 ++ fv b1 ++ fv_context C'
  | C_IfCondition C' y n _ => fv_context C' ++ fv y ++ fv n
  | C_IfThen c C' n _ => fv c ++ fv_context C' ++ fv n
  | C_IfElse c y C' _ => fv c ++ fv y ++ fv_context C'
  end.

Lemma subst_atoms_plug_fresh : forall C x u,
  ~ In x (fv_context C) ->
  subst_atoms (extend x u tm_fvar) (plug C (tm_fvar x)) = plug C u.
Proof by induction on C; simpl; ...
```
Each case: subst_atoms of a compound term = compound of substs; use IH and the freshness of x in the fixed terms to show subst_atoms (extend x u tm_fvar) t = t (via subst_atoms_extend_fresh + subst_atoms_id: subst_atoms (extend x u tm_fvar) t = subst_atoms tm_fvar t = t, given x ∉ fv t).

A helper:
```
Lemma subst_atoms_id_on : forall x u t, ~ In x (fv t) -> subst_atoms (extend x u tm_fvar) t = t.
Proof.
  intros. rewrite subst_atoms_extend_fresh by exact H. apply subst_atoms_id.
Qed.
```

Then the plug lemma. Let me write it with explicit cases.

**The erasure machinery**:

```
Fixpoint nested_le (kappa : security) (T : ty) : Prop :=
  security_le (security_of T) kappa /\
  match T with
  | Ty_Unit _ => True
  | Ty_Sum T1 T2 _ | Ty_Prod T1 T2 _ | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 _ => nested_le kappa T1 /\ nested_le kappa T2
  end.

Lemma transparent_at_nested_le : forall kappa T, transparent_at kappa T -> nested_le kappa T.
Proof. intros kappa T. induction T; simpl; intros [Hle Hrec]; split; auto.
  - (* Sum *) destruct Hrec as [H1 H2]; split; auto.
  - (* Prod *) ...
  - (* Arrow *) ...
Qed.

Fixpoint all_indirect_flows (l : label) (T : ty) : Prop :=
  flows_to (security_of T).(indirect_reader) l /\
  match T with
  | Ty_Unit _ => True
  | Ty_Sum T1 T2 _ | Ty_Prod T1 T2 _ | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 _ =>
      all_indirect_flows l T1 /\ all_indirect_flows l T2
  end.

Lemma nested_le_all_indirect : forall l kappa T,
  nested_le kappa T -> flows_to kappa.(indirect_reader) l -> all_indirect_flows l T.
Proof by induction on T...
```

The issue: `rewrite IHC; auto` — the IHC premise needs `~ In x (fv_context C)`, but Hx : ~ In x (fv_context C ++ fv t). `auto` can't derive it. I need explicit derivations:
```
    apply IHC. intro H; apply Hx; apply in_app_iff; left; exact H.
```
And for the fixed terms: `apply subst_atoms_extend_id; intro H; apply Hx; apply in_app_iff; right; exact H`.

Note the shapes vary per constructor: for C_AppLeft, fv_context = fv_context C' ++ fv t. So:
- IHC premise: In x (fv_context C') → in the left part.
- fixed term t: In x (fv t) → right part.

For C_AppRight: fv_context = fv t ++ fv_context C' → IHC premise: right part; t: left.

For CaseScrutinee: fv_context C' ++ fv b1 ++ fv b2 = (fv_context C' ++ fv b1) ++ fv b2. IHC: left of the outer app, then left again. b1: left then right; b2: right.

Hmm, `auto` with in_app_iff might handle some. Let me write helper tactics/lemmas:

```
Lemma not_in_app_l : forall (l1 l2 : list atom) x, ~ In x (l1 ++ l2) -> ~ In x l1.
Proof. intros l1 l2 x H Hin; apply H; apply in_app_iff; left; exact Hin. Qed.
Lemma not_in_app_r : forall (l1 l2 : list atom) x, ~ In x (l1 ++ l2) -> ~ In x l2.
Proof. intros l1 l2 x H Hin; apply H; apply in_app_iff; right; exact Hin. Qed.
```
Then each case:
- Abs: `rewrite IHC; auto` — Hx : ~ In x (fv_context C) (since fv_context (C_Abs _ C' _) = fv_context C') ✓ auto.

  - (* AppLeft *) simpl. rewrite IHC. 2: { apply not_in_app_l; exact Hx. }

  ```

Cases and their fv_context shapes:
1. C_Hole: [].
2. C_Abs T C' k: fv_context C'.
3. C_AppLeft C' t r: fv_context C' ++ fv t.
4. C_AppRight t C' r: fv t ++ fv_context C'.
5. C_PairLeft C' t k: fv_context C' ++ fv t.
6. C_PairRight t C' k: fv t ++ fv_context C'.
7. C_Fst C' r: fv_context C'.
8. C_Snd: fv_context C'.
9. C_Inl, C_Inr: fv_context C'.
10. C_CaseScrutinee C' b1 b2 r: fv_context C' ++ fv b1 ++ fv b2.
11. C_CaseLeft t0 C' b2 r: fv t0 ++ fv_context C' ++ fv b2.

第 126 次思考 | 原始 trace 第 390 行
========================================================================

Everything compiles now (with the theorem still Admitted). Now let me write the final theorem proof.

The theorem:
```
Theorem noninterference : forall (C : program_context) (t1 t2 : tm)
    (T_secret T_result : ty),
  has_type empty t1 T_secret ->
  has_type empty t2 T_secret ->
  context_has_type C T_secret T_result ->
  ground T_result ->
  transparent T_result ->
  ~ flows_to (security_of T_secret).(indirect_reader)
      (security_of T_result).(indirect_reader) ->
  same_result (plug C t1) (plug C t2).
Proof.
  intros C t1 t2 T_secret T_result Ht1 Ht2 HC Hg Htr Hnflow.
  apply flows_to_false in Hnflow.
  destruct Hnflow as [Hsec Hres].
  destruct HC as [L Hplug].
  set (x := fresh_atom (L ++ fv_context C)).
  assert (HxL : ~ In x L) by (unfold x; apply fresh_atom_not_in_l).
  assert (HxC : ~ In x (fv_context C)) by (unfold x; apply fresh_atom_not_in_r).
  (* TR at secret type *)
  assert (HsecTR : TR Low T_secret t1 t2).
  { apply VR_vacuous. rewrite Hsec. simpl. tauto. }
  (* env_lc *)
  assert (Hlc1 : env_lc (extend x t1 tm_fvar)).
  { apply env_lc_extend.
    - apply env_lc_id.
    - apply has_type_locally_closed. exact Ht1. }
  assert (Hlc2 : env_lc (extend x t2 tm_fvar)).
  { apply env_lc_extend.
    - apply env_lc_id.
    - apply has_type_locally_closed. exact Ht2. }
  (* env_rel *)
  assert (Henv : env_rel Low (update empty x T_secret)
                   (extend x t1 tm_fvar) (extend x t2 tm_fvar)).
  { intros y U Hlk. unfold update, lookup_context in Hlk. simpl in Hlk.
    destruct (Nat.eqb y x) eqn:E.
    - apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst y. injection Hlk as ->.
      rewrite extend_eq. rewrite extend_eq. exact HsecTR.
    - discriminate Hlk. }
  (* fundamental theorem *)
  pose proof (fundamental _ _ _ (Hplug x HxL) Low _ _ Hlc1 Hlc2 Henv) as HtrR.
  rewrite (subst_atoms_plug_fresh C x t1 HxC) in HtrR.
  rewrite (subst_atoms_plug_fresh C x t2 HxC) in HtrR.
  (* all indirect flows *)
  assert (Hall : all_indirect_flows Low T_result).
  { apply nested_le_all_indirect.
    - apply transparent_at_nested_le. exact Htr.
    - rewrite Hres. apply flows_to_refl. }
  (* conclude *)
  intros v1 v2 E1 E2.
  unfold TR in HtrR.
  apply (VR_erase Low T_result Hg Hall).
  apply HtrR; assumption.
Qed.
```

The proof architecture:
- Binary logical relation `VR l T v1 v2` on values, parameterized by an observer level `l`, guarded by whether the type's `indirect_reader` flows to `l`.
- `TR` = all completed evaluations lead to related values.
- Key lemmas: vacuity (`VR_vacuous`), protection (`VR_protect_ty`, `VR_protect_value_same`, `VR_protect_value`, `TR_protect`), subtyping monotonicity (`VR_sub`, `TR_sub`), multi-step decompositions for every syntactic elimination form.
- Fundamental theorem via simultaneous atom substitution with locally-closed environments and the env relation.
- Erasure lemma `VR_erase`: relatedness at a ground, "transparent" result type with all nested indirect readers flowing to `Low` implies erased-value equality.
- Final theorem: from `~flows_to secret.ind result.ind` derive secret.ind = High and result.ind = Low; the secret type's relation at Low is vacuous, so any two well-typed secret terms are related; the fundamental theorem transports this through the context; `VR_erase` yields `same_result`.
